市場疑慮拖累美股，短線聚焦 Fed 決策與財報
2026 年 9 月 15 日
一、 9/14 美股下挫、美債殖利率曲線熊市趨平，且油價大漲
1. 股票指數：
⚫ 道瓊工業指數下跌 0.29%，收 52,421.20 點；
⚫ S&P 500 指數下挫 0.48%，收 7,619.98 點；
⚫ 那斯達克綜合指數下跌 0.56%，收 26,186.41 點；
⚫ 費城半導體指數下跌 5.86%，收 11,131.28 點。
2. 當日重要個股表現：
⚫ 在科技類股方面，輝達下跌約 3.4%、美光下跌 5.3%、邁威爾下跌 7.3%；

⚫ 通訊類股方面，Netflix 上漲 3.8%、Take-Two 上漲 3.5%、Fox 上漲 4.6%；
⚫ 軟體類股方面 ，ServiceNow、Adobe、Workday 等軟體股則上漲約 4% 至
7.4%。
3. 美國債市漲跌不一、殖利率曲線走勢熊市趨平。2 年期美國公債殖利率上升 3.24 個基
點至 4.66%，10 年期殖利率上升 2.06 個基點至 4.99%，10 年期殖利率一度升破 5%，
而 30 年期殖利率則下跌約 0.76 個基點至 5.34%。短率上升，反映市場對未來美國聯
準會升息的預期。
4. 油價因中東衝突與供給風險再度升高而上漲 ，WTI 原油上漲 1.81%，收在每桶 101.86
美元，布蘭特原油則上漲 1.56%，收在 106.24 美元。
二、 下跌原因分析
1. AI 開發放緩疑慮，壓抑硬體需求預期：Anthropic 執行長 Dario Amodei 於上週末
呼籲放慢前沿 AI 能力提升速度，並提出第三方安全評估與業界協調方案，使 AI 擴

不另行通知。
張節奏成為新的不確定因素。我們研判，半導體位於 AI 建置支出的上游，股價對新
增算力投資的成長速度尤其敏感。即使既有訂單未立即改變，只要市場降低對未來數
季增速的期待，GPU、高頻寬記憶體及網通設備供應鏈的評價便可能先行調整；目前
仍須區分投資預期降溫與實際訂單下修。
2. 中東能源設施遇襲，油價推升通膨風險：沙烏地阿拉伯能源設施及中東船舶遇襲，引
發供應疑慮，布蘭特原油期貨 9 月 14 日結算價升至每桶 106.24 美元。油價持續偏
高可能透過運輸與生產成本以及民眾實質購買力影響企業獲利；若進一步傳導至通膨

預期，將延長貨幣政策維持緊縮的時間，對股市評價形成壓力。
3. 十年美債殖利率一度突破 5%，評價與融資承壓：美國 10 年期公債殖利率盤中突破
5%，為 2023 年以來首見。殖利率上升提高未來獲利的折現率，對依賴長期成長預
期支撐評價的股票較不利。另一方面，資料中心若仰賴借款擴張，融資成本上升也會
提高投資報酬門檻，可能影響新增建置計畫，形成 AI 供應鏈面臨的另一項壓力。
4. 通膨月增回升 ， 升息前觀望氣氛加重 ：BLS 於 9 月 11 日公布 ，8 月 CPI 月增 0.4%
，高於 7 月的 0.1%，核心 CPI 月增率也由 0.2% 升至 0.3%。我們認為，短期物
價動能回升，加上能源價格偏高，提高聯準會維持緊縮政策的壓力。投資人除了評估
當次升息，也須重新衡量後續利率高點與維持時間，增加 FOMC 會議前高波動股票
的調整壓力。
5. 成長與評價雙重重估，放大半導體跌幅：本波股市回檔壓力同時來自獲利成長預期與
市場願給的評價倍數。AI 開發放緩疑慮影響未來營收想像，殖利率上行則壓低未來獲

利的現值。當股價已反映較高成長期待，只要新增訊息不如預期，即使當期營收仍強
勁，也可能出現明顯修正。後續應觀察獲利上修能否抵銷評價收縮，不能僅以跌幅大
就認定股價便宜。

不另行通知。
三、市場看法
1. 龍頭財報仍具成長，支持中期正向看法：輝達 8 月 26 日公布的 2027 會計年度第
二季資料中心營收達 890 億美元、年增 117%，並預估第三季總營收 1,080 億美元
、上下浮動 2%。博通 9 月 2 日公布的 2026 會計年度第三季 AI 半導體營收為
167 億美元、年增 221%，並預估第四季達 217 億美元。上述實績與公司展望，是
我們對 AI 核心供應鏈維持中期正向看法的依據；惟這些財測發布於本次事件之前，
仍須追蹤後續更新。
2. 放緩方案尚待落地，需求影響須分項評估：Anthropic 執行長 Amodei 明確表示，放

緩不代表停止模型訓練，但也提出研議訓練算力等限制，因此實際影響取決於後續執
行方式。既有模型的商業使用、推論與安全測試仍可能帶來算力需求，惟能否抵銷前
沿訓練投資的放緩，必須由使用量、訂單與收入驗證。此時適合保留成長觀點，同時
提高對支出變化的敏感度。
3. 科技股仍有輪動，軟體與半導體強弱分明：9/14 當日企業軟體與資安股逆勢上漲，顯
示科技股內部仍有輪動與受惠程度的差異。我們認為，若前沿 AI 開發放緩，部分既
有軟體業者面臨的競爭及產品替代壓力可能減輕；AI 安全疑慮升高，也可能強化資安
、權限控管及企業治理需求。相較之下，半導體更直接承受新增建置支出預期變化。
因此，9/14 當日費半重挫尚不足以推論整體科技需求同步轉弱；後續宜比較訂單能見
度、訂閱收入、獲利與現金流，單日相對強勢仍須由基本面延續性驗證。
4. 升息機率約九成，會後政策態度才是關鍵：依 Reuters 於美東 9 月 14 日收盤報導
引述的 CME FedWatch 資料，本次升息一碼的機率約 90%。若美東 9 月 16 日

FOMC 確實升息 0.25 個百分點，利率區間將升至 3.75% 至 4.00%，當次升息已
大致反映於市場預期。我們認為，會後聲明及主席記者會對未來利率政策的態度才是
關鍵：是否暗示還需升息、利率須維持高檔多久，以及是否擔心能源漲價向核心通膨

擴散，並須搭配點陣圖判讀。若後續緊縮訊號未超出預期、長債殖利率趨穩，有利評
價修復；若利率路徑較預期更高、更持久，科技股仍可能承壓。
5. 通膨改善仍不均衡，避免過早判定轉折：8 月核心 CPI 年增率為 2.4%，低於 7 月
的 2.5%，但其月增率由 0.2% 升至 0.3%，顯示年增放緩與短期動能回升並存。後
續須觀察能源漲價是否向服務價格與通膨預期擴散。若油價回穩、核心物價再度降溫
，利率壓力可望緩和；若能源衝擊持續，股市調整時間可能拉長。
四、投資建議
1. 短線聚焦 Fed 決策與 10 月美股財報，科技股中期向上趨勢料不致有變：我們目前仍

維持 AI 核心供應鏈中期正向看法，較看好能將營收成長轉化為獲利與現金流的企業
，但宜密切觀察本週的 FOMC 會議結果與 10 月中將起跑的美股財報。若雲端業者維
持資本支出、晶片公司財測穩定，且 FOMC 此次會議未釋出超預期緊縮訊號，有利
市場信心修復；然而，若出現廣泛支出下修、訂單遞延，或油價與殖利率持續上行，
則應拉長觀察期並重新檢視通膨與利率對於股市動能前景的影響。
2. 3~10 年美國公債/投資級債為債市首選：目前美國的通膨數據仍具韌性，且就業市場
穩健，使年底前市場升息預期再度升溫。市場對美國財政赤字、公債供給及長期通膨
仍要求較高風險補償，30 年期殖利率與短天期利差仍維持高檔，顯示期限溢酬尚未收
斂，考量當前 3～10 年期美國公債與投資級債能鎖定目前較高息收，且其受長端財政
風險影響也相對較小，故現階段3～10 年期美國公債及投資級債的風險報酬結構相對
較佳。

金融市場投資週報                 2026 年 10 月 13 日
上週重點訊息
◼ TOPIX 啟動大規模改革：日本交易所集團 10 月 7 日公布 TOPIX 調整結果，符合新制納入標
準的成分股將由 9 月底的 1,636 檔降至 986 檔，另有 35 檔股票新納入。現有成分股中，共
有 683 檔未符合流動性及浮動市值等標準，這些股票將自 2026 年 10 月底起逐步降低權
重，並於 2027 年 10 月重新評估，屆時仍未達標者將於 2028 年 7 月底降至零。此次改革有
助於改善 TOPIX 成分股的流動性及投資價值，並促使企業更加重視市場評價。

◼ 巴西總統選舉波索納洛首輪領先魯拉：巴西最高選舉法院 10 月 5 日公布首輪投票結果，參
議員弗拉維奧・波索納洛取得 47.03%的有效票，現任總統魯拉獲得 45.16%，兩人均未取得
過半票數，將於 10 月 25 日進行第二輪決選。若最終由波索納洛當選將相較有利於巴西股市
表現。
◼ IEA 支持加速釋出既有儲油：國際能源署（IEA）10 月 7 日表示，會員國支持加快釋出 3 月
已承諾提供的儲油，並將優先增加柴油供應。截至目前，會員國已釋出約 3.25 億桶儲油；若
剩餘承諾全數執行，可再向市場提供約 1 億桶供應，有助緩解運輸業與農業面臨的燃料成本
壓力。隨各國持續釋出戰略儲備石油，將有利於在短期內壓抑全球油價進一步上行。
市場展望
◼ 標普 500 獲利預估連續上修，資訊科技為主要推升來源：標普 500 企業獲利預估連續兩季上
修，其中資訊科技產業為主要改善來源，反映 AI、雲端運算及資料中心需求仍然強勁。雖然
美國資料中心仍面臨電力與基礎設施瓶頸，但建置規模持續擴大，AI 投資也逐步延伸至記憶

體、網路設備、資安及企業應用等領域。因此，預期科技類股的獲利動能仍具支撐，具 AI 供
應鏈優勢的台股與日股亦可望延續相關成長動能。
◼ 中天期債券可望兼顧息收與利率風險：美國經濟仍具韌性，加上政府與企業發債供給維持高
檔，可能使市場利率維持在較高水準。雖然投資級債信用利差位於相對低檔，但企業獲利、
現金流及償債能力仍具支撐，較高的美國公債基準利率也使其利息收益維持一定水準。短天
期債券面臨利率下降後的再投資風險，長天期債券則較易受到公債供給及利率波動影響，因
此中天期美國公債及投資級債可望在息收與利率風險之間維持較為平衡的表現。

戶 預 付 款 協 議，將 由 NVIDIA 預 先 支 付 款 項，支 持 京 元 電 及 美 國 全 資 子 公 司
AeternZ Semiconductor Testing Corporation 在 美 國 建 置 測 試 產 能 ， 未 來 再 以
提 供 測 試 服 務 抵 減 預 付 款。京 元 電 同 步 宣 布，因 合 約 架 構 調 整，取 消 原 先 對
美 國 子 公 司 提 供 的 3 億 美 元 背 書 保 證 額 度 。 
京 元 電 此 次簽約 對 象 為 NVIDIA International, Inc. ， NVIDIA 預 付 款 項 ， 京
元 電 及 其 美 國 全 資 子 公 司 將 在 美 國 建 置 測 試 產 能 ， 為 NVIDIA 保 留 產 能 並
提 供 測 試 服 務，預 付 款 則 以 未 來 服 務 款 項 抵 減。此 合 作 並 非 股 權 投 資 或 合 資
模 式，而 是 在 既 有 客 戶 關 係 下，透 過 客 戶 提 前 提 供 資 金，支 持 供 應 商 擴 充 產
能 並 確 保 未 來 測 試 資 源 。 
京 元 電 董 事 會 已 於 07/ 2026 決 議 通 過 美 國 投 資 設 廠 案，規 劃 在 美 國 設 立 孫 公
司 並 興 建 新 廠 ， 投 資 金 額 以 14 億美元 (約 新 台 幣 450 億元 )為 上 限 。 公 司 當
時 表 示，此 投 資 主 要 因 應 未 來 營 運 需 求 及 全 球 供 應 鏈 布 局，後 續 將 依 設 廠 進
度 與 資 金 需 求 逐 步 投 入 ， 資 金 來 源 以 自 有 資 金 或 借 款 等 方 式 支 應 。 
此 次 協 議 有 助 於 提 高 新 增 產 能 的 需 求 能 見 度，並 降 低 部 分 初 期 資 金 壓 力。不
過，由 於 預 付 款 金 額、實 際 保 留 產 能 規 模 及 相 關 訂 單 金 額 尚 未 公 開，現 階 段
仍 無 法 量 化 對 營 收 與 獲 利 的 貢 獻；短 期 營 運 表 現 仍 取 決 於 既 有 產 能 擴 充、新
晶 片 測 試 需 求 及 美 國 廠 建 置 進 度 。 
NVIDIA 在 07/2026 已和 Amkor(AMKR US) 簽 訂 預 付 款 協 議 ， 約 15 億美
元 ：  
值 得 注 意 的 是 ，NVIDIA 於 07/ 2026 與 Amkor 簽訂約 15 億 美 元 的 多 年 期 先
進 封 裝 與 測 試 合 作 協 議，包 括 正 在 亞 利 桑 那 州 建 設 的 生 產 設 施。Amkor 目前
已為 NVIDIA 資 料 中 心 處 理 器 、 網 通 晶 片 組 及 加 速 運 算 系 統 提 供 先 進 封 裝
服務 。  
Amkor 於 2Q 26 法 說 會 表 示 ， 相 關 預 付 款 預 計 於 2027 年 收 取 ， 並 隨 後 續 提
供 服 務 逐 步 抵 減 ， 依 實 際 生 產 規 模 及 服 務 提 供 速 度 ， 預 估 合 作 期 間 約5~10
年 。 此 案 例 顯 示 ，NVIDIA 透 過 預 付 款 支 持 封 測 廠 擴 充 美 國 產 能 ， 並 非 短 期
採 購 安 排 ， 而 可 能 是 配 合 長 期 產 能 建 置 及 技 術 發 展 的 合 作 模 式 。 
京 元 電 此 次 亦 採 取和 Amkor 相 似 的 方 式 ，客 戶 預 付 款 、 建 置 美 國 產 能 並 以
未 來 測 試 服 務 款 項 抵 減。 Amkor 案 例 可 作 為 評 估 合 作 期 間 的 參 考。  
2 家 不 同 之 處，京 元 電 主 要 受 惠 於 高 階 測 試 需 求；Amkor 則 涉 及 先 進 封 裝 與

測 試。兩 者 都 能 提 高 產 能 需 求 能 見 度，但 實 際 營 收 與 獲 利 貢 獻 仍 取 決 於 產 能
開 出 、 訂 單 規 模 及 服 務 條 件 。 
外 媒 報 導 京 元 電 美 國 子 公 司預 計 落 腳 德 州 沃 斯 堡 市(Fort Worth )：  
在 美 國 設 廠 地 點 方 面， 外 媒 於10/ 2026 報 導， 京 元 電 全 資 美 國 子 公 司AeternZ
正 評 估 於 德 州 沃 斯 堡(Fort Worth )設 立 半 導 體 測 試 廠，初 步 規 劃 投 資 約 13 億
美 元 ， 其 中 約 10 億 美 元 用 於 不 動 產 、3 億 美 元 用 於 設 備 ， 選 址 涉 及 West 
Everman Parkway 一 處 約 23 英 畝 的 土 地 。 沃 斯 堡 市 議 會 預 計 於 10 /27/2026
審 議 相 關 租 稅 優 惠，但 目 前 尚 不 能 視 為 京 元 電 已 正 式 決 定 設 廠 地 點，仍 須 觀
察 後 續 選 址 及 投 資 進 度。若 該 地 點 獲 選，德州相對 台 積 電 所 在 的 亞 利 桑 那 州
鳳 凰 城 聚 落 ，地 緣 上 相 對 鄰 近 AI 伺 服 器 供 應 鏈 ，代 表 AI 晶 片 完 成 測 試 後，
可 就 近 送 往 當 地 代 工 廠 ， 進 行 整 機 組 裝 與 系 統 測 試 。不 過 ， 廠 房 建 置 、 設 備
進 駐 及 客 戶 認 證 仍 需 時 間 ， 實 際 貢 獻 時 點 取 決 於 建 廠 進 度 與 測 試 需 求 。 
AI 晶 片 由 傳 統 單 一 系 統 單 晶 片(SoC) 朝 Chiplet 及 異 質 整 合 發 展， 封 測 複 雜
度 提 升 ， IC 設 計 廠 和 封 測 廠 合 作 深 化：  
隨著 AI 晶 片 由 傳 統 單 一 系 統 單 晶 片(SoC ) 朝 Chiplet 及 異 質 整 合 發 展 ， 半 導
體 產 業 的 競 爭 已 不 再 侷 限 於 晶 片 設 計 與 先 進 製 程，也 延 伸 至 先 進 封 裝、晶 粒
間 高 速 互 連、散 熱 及 測 試 驗 證 等 環 節。晶 片 架 構 複 雜 度 提 高，使 封 裝 與 測 試
成 為 影 響 產 品 效 能 、 成 本 及 量 產 能 力 的 重 要 因 素 。 
對 京 元 電 而 言，若 能 持 續 參 與 NVIDIA 新 晶 片 的 測 試 開 發 與 驗 證，並 承 接 後
續 量 產 測 試，有 助 於 深 化 客 戶 關 係 及 鞏 固 高 階 測 試 業 務 的 競 爭 優 勢；但 此 次
預付款協議本身並未改變雙方的股權關係，合作價值仍須透過後續訂單與
產 能 利 用 率 驗 證 。 
相 較 之 下 ， 力 成(6239 TT) 與 Broadcom (AVGO US) 規 劃 在 新 加 坡 成 立 先 進 封
裝 合 資 公 司 ， 發 展 扇 出 型 面 板 級 封 裝 （FOPLP ）及 重 布 線 層 （RDL ） 等 相 關
技術，屬於 IC 設 計 公 司 直 接 參 與 封 裝 製 造 平 台 投 資 的 模 式 。 此 合 作 不 僅 涉
及 產 能 配 置，也 涵 蓋 新 技 術 開 發 與 量 產 導 入，反 映 當 先 進 封 裝 成 為 影 響 晶 片
效 能 、 互 連 密 度 及 量 產 成 本 的 關 鍵 環 節 ，IC 設 計 公 司 可 能 更 積 極 參 與 封 裝
技 術 與 產 能 布 局。對 力 成 而 言，若 能 順 利 完 成 製 程 驗 證、良 率 提 升 及 量 產 導
入，有 機 會 拓 展 新 的 技 術 平 台 與 業 務 成 長 機 會；惟 仍 須 觀 察 客 戶 訂 單、量 產
進 度 、 資 本 支 出 及 投 資 報 酬 率 。 
整 體 而 言 ，NVIDIA 與 京 元 電 、Amkor 的 合 作 ， 主 要 是 透 過 客 戶 預 付 款 支 持
產 能 建 置 ， 強 化 供 應 商 的 需 求 與 資 金 能 見 度 ；Broadcom 與 力 成 的 合 作 ， 則
是 透 過 共 同 投 資 發 展 新 的 先 進 封 裝 技 術 平 台 。 兩 者 反 映AI 半 導 體 產 業 的 合
作 模 式 正 逐 步 深 化，但 資 金 安 排、技 術 參 與 程 度 及 投 資 風 險 並 不 相 同。投 資
上，京 元 電 應 關 注 預 付 款 規 模、美 國 新 廠 投 產 及 高 階 測 試 訂 單 成 長；力 成 則
應 觀 察 先 進 封 裝 技 術 商 業 化、量 產 良 率 與 合 資 事 業 的 投 資 報 酬。京 元 電 此 次
協 議 對 中 長 期 業 務 發 展 具 正 面 意 義，但 在 實 際 財 務 貢 獻 尚 未 明 朗 前，宜 視 為
客 戶 關 係 深 化 及 產 能 布 局 的 正 面 訊 號，而 非 直 接 上 修 獲 利 預 估 的 依 據。由於
目 前 京 元 電 評 價 合 理 ，投 資 建 議 Neutral 。

4 群益投顧 
  【 投 資 評 等 說 明 】 
  評等  定義  
  強力買進 (Strong Buy)  首 次 評 等 潛 在 上 漲 空 間≥ 35%  
  買進 (Buy)  15%≤ 首 次 評 等 潛 在 上 漲 空 間<35%  
  區間操作 (Trading Buy)  5%≤ 首 次 評 等 潛 在 上 漲 空 間<15%

中立 (Neutral)  
無 法 由 基 本 面 給 予 投 資 評 等 
預 期 近 期 股 價 將 處 於 盤 整 
建 議 降 低 持 股

【 投 資 評 等 調 整 機 制 】 
  投 資 評 等 係「 首 次 」給 予 特 定 個 股 投 資 評 等 時，其 前 一 日 收 盤 價 相 較3 個月
目 標 價 之 潛 在 上 漲 空 間 計 算 而 得 。 個 股 投 資 評 等 分 為四 個 等 級 ， 定 義 如 上 。
爾 後 的 投 資 評 等係 依 循 「 首 次 評 等 」 ， 直 到 停 止 推 薦 。  
「 強 力 買 進 」、「 買 進 」及 「 區 間 操 作 」均有 upside 目 標 價 。差 別 僅 在 於 ，
首 次 評 等 時 潛 在 報 酬 率 不 同 。 「 中 立 」 則 無 目 標 價 。 
一 旦 我 們 給 予 特 定 個 股「 強 力 買 進 」、「 買 進 」或「 區 間 操 作 」之 投 資 評 等，
就 是 責 任 的 開 始，爾 後 將 透 過 各 式 研 究 報 告 作 定 期 性、持 續 性 追 蹤 基 本 面 及
股 價 變 化 ， 直 到 停 止 推 薦 。 
停 止 推 薦 情 境 ： 
1.  達 目 標 價 。 
2.  雖 未 達 原 訂 目 標 價 ， 但 檢 視 基 本 面 、 訊 息 面 、 籌 碼 面 等 多 方 訊 息 ， 研
判 股 價 上 漲 空 間 已 然 有 限 ， 將 適 時 出 具 降 評 報 告 。 
3.  推 薦 後 股 價 不 漲 反 跌 ， 亦 將 出 具 降 評 報 告 。

【免責聲明】  
  本 研 究 報 告 僅 提 供 予 特 定 人 之 客 戶 作 為 參 考 資 料「 非 經 同 意 不 得 轉 載 」 。我
們 並 不 確 保 此 資 訊 的 完 整 性 與 正 確 性，投 資 人 應 了 解，報 告 中 有 關 未 來 預 測
之 陳 述 可 能 不 會 實 現，因 而 不 應 被 依 賴。而 且 此 報 告 並 非 根 據 特 定 投 資 目 的
或 依 預 定 對 象 之 財 務 狀 況 所 撰 寫 出 來 的，因 此，此 研 究 報 告 的 目 的，既 非 對
投 資 人 於 買 賣 證 券、選 擇 權、期 貨 或 其 他 證 券 相 關 之 衍 生 性 商 品 提 供 詢 價 服
務，亦 非 作 為 進 行 交 易 的 要 約。投 資 人 應 注 意 到 相 關 證 券 之 價 值 及 收 益，可
能會有無預警地上升或下降，產生投資回報金額可能比原始投資來得少的
情形。

## Document 105: 261018_ms_OCP-takeaways

Source PDF: all report pdf/261018_ms_OCP-takeaways.pdf

### Page 1

Greater China Technology Hardware
Asia Pacific
Industry View In-Line
CPO/NPO and micro     VCSEL based   optical solutions are the
focus for networking, while HVDC takes center stage for power.
Higher bandwidth and better power efficiency: In the networking space, the
migration into higher bandwidth continues, as players like Accton and Celestica
showcased their 1.6T network switches. We think a bigger theme is the effort to
improve the power efficiency of current mainstream pluggable optical solutions.

First off, one well-discussed approach is the adoption CPO or NPO, and it seems the
latter is gaining more attention due to the technical challenges in the former. Spec
standardization, such as CPX connectors, could further accelerate the proliferation,
in our view. Secondly, copper could still help, as Credo has its 1.6T AEC cables for
inter-tray transmission while many other vendors (Amphenol, TE Connectivity,
Molex, etc.) have their 448G CPC solutions for in-tray transmission. Lastly, the micro

VCSEL based optical modules from Credo and ALAB represent another power
efficient solution (12W/end at 1,6T), which has reliability comparable to copper
(100M hours of MTBF/MTBI) for a transmission distance of 50-100m. We believe
this micro VCSEL based solution could potentially replace the AEC for shorter
distance transmission when copper hits the physical limit in the medium term and
be integrated with the CPO architecture in the longer term. In sum, we believe the

above-mentioned solutions are likely to co-exist for different use cases.
800VDC for power delivery taking the center stage: While the Vera Rubin NVL72
rack already has the in-rack power shelf reference design (110KW/shelf at 3+1
redundancy), Nvidia unveiled another reference design this time, using one 660KW
800VDC power rack to support two Vera Rubin NVL72 racks. Moreover, we also
saw a few ±400VDC reference designs as well. Our conversations with companies

on the exhibition floor indicate that the HVDC adoption should start from late
2026, but likely at a small scale extending into 2027. The next checkpoint will be
Vera Rubin Ultra racks, where increasing power consumption and more hardware
devices for inter-rack scale-up network might take up more pace on the rack, though
the liquid cooled power shelf  should ease the space budget a bit. We believe both
solutions are progressing and will closely monitor the dynamics.

Stock picks: We believe that Bizlink (3665.TW) should be a beneficiary of the
proliferation of the micro VCSEL based optical solution, as it is the assembly partner
for Credo and potentially others as well. Chroma (2360.TW) should see drivers
from power testing, CPU/GPU/TPU SL T/FT, and photonics testing (pluggable/
CPO)tools. Delta( (2308.TW) and Lite-On (2301.TW) should continue to grow with
the increasing power consumption of AI clusters, but more meaningful growth

communications with a subject company, public appearances
and trading securities held by a research analyst account.

2026 OCP Global Summit
Networking dealing with higher bandwidth and better power

1.6T network switch and 1.6T transceiver/AEC
CPO remains one of the focus in the networking space during the OCP Global Summit.
Edgecore and  Celestica showcased its 1.6T network switches based on Tomahawk 6 ASIC,
both for the regular model and the CPO model. Moreover, Edgecore also had its OCS
switch model in the booth, which is based on its own LCOS technology. These

demonstrate that both have the capability to accommodate the customer demand for
network switches based on the optical architecture in the future.
On the cable side, Credo showcased its 1.6T AEC, but did not comment on the roadmap of
its 3.2T AEC. Our checks in the exhibition with industry players indicate that the 3.2T AEC
might be a very challenging project, though efforts are still in place.

CPX and NPX all marching forward
Though CPO remains as the holy grail in the space, its yield issue and lower serviceability
still present challenges to the wider adoption in the near term. We believe that the efforts
will continue, but the industry is simultaneously pursuing other more practical solutions

to sort out the power consumption issue of the existing pluggable transceivers,  including
NPO (near-packaged optics), CPC (co-packaged copper) and NPC (near-packaged copper)
Compared to CPO, NPO compromises on the power consumption, but in exchange it eases
the stringent requirement on alignment and serviceability. Given its better technology
readiness and more mature supply chain, we think it might see good traction in the
coming years.

VCSEL based optical solutions gaining more attention
In addition to CPO/NPO/CPC/NPC designs, we see the micro VCSEL based optical

and slow architecture for a relatively short transmission distance of 50-100m. It can

current generation can enable 32Gbps of bandwidth per lane and the 100-200Gbps of
bandwidth per lane is in the roadmap.

In sum, different solutions of data transmission are emerging, trying to improve the power

Power becoming another bottleneck in the AI deployment
HVDC adoption emerging from Vera Rubin
In the power space, HVDC has been one of the key focuses in the AI cluster, as the rapid
increase in AI rack power density is creating growing challenges for conventional power-
distribution architectures. As rack-level power consumption rising from tens of kilowatts
to several hundreds of KWs, electrical losses, cable size, thermal management
requirements, and overall system complexity increase significantly. Under traditional low-

voltage power architectures, delivering larger amounts of power requires proportionally
higher current, which increases resistive losses and places greater stress on cables,
connectors, busbars, and power-conversion equipment.
At the same time, every power-conversion stage introduces efficiency losses and
generates additional heat. As AI deployments scale into multi-megawatt and eventually
gigawatt-level campuses, even small efficiency improvements can translate into

### Page 10

M
Idea
10
meaningful reductions in operating costs, cooling requirements, and infrastructure
investment. Consequently, the industry is increasingly focused on power architectures
that can reduce conversion stages, lower current levels, and improve overall power-
delivery efficiency.
Against this backdrop, high-voltage direct current (HVDC) is emerging as one of the most
important architectural upgrades for next-generation AI infrastructure. By increasing

distribution voltage, HVDC allows the same amount of power to be delivered at a lower
current, reducing conduction losses and improving system efficiency. Higher-voltage
architectures also improve scalability for ultra-high-density rack deployments by
alleviating some of the physical and thermal constraints associated with conventional
power-distribution systems.
Exhibit 18: Better power efficiency for 800VDC architecture
Source: Delta
Exhibit 19: Better efficiency leads to cost savings

Source: Delta
In a joint keynote speech by Delta and Nvidia, the roadmap illustrates the industry's
gradual migration toward higher-voltage power architectures. Earlier AI server generations
primarily utilized conventional power-delivery schemes centered around rack-level 48V
distribution. As rack power requirements increased with Blackwell-generation systems,
industry participants began evaluating higher-voltage architectures capable of supporting

substantially greater power density. Looking ahead, the industry is increasingly converging
around 800VDC as a potential next-generation standard for large-scale AI deployments,
particularly as rack-level power consumption approaches several hundred kilowatts. The
transition reflects the need to improve power-distribution efficiency while supporting the
continued growth of AI computing infrastructure.
Though we also saw the 800VDC power rack reference design for the Vera Rubin rack in a

1-to-2 configuration (one 660KW power rack supporting two Vera Rubin racks), we think
the adoption of this solution might be relatively low, since the Vera Rubin rack also has a
reference design for the in-rack power shelf design

### Page 11

M
Idea
Morgan Stanley Research 11
Exhibit 20: 800VDC roadmap
Source: Delta
Exhibit 21: 800VDC reference design for VR
Source: Delta
Liquid cooled power shelf on the horizon
The next key check point to watch should be the Vera Rubin Ultra generation, as the
power consumption per rack shall continue to increase and there might be more hardware
devices to enable the inter-rack scale up network, both could take up more space on the
IT rack. That said, the power shelf will shift from air cooling into liquid cooling, which

could narrow the power shelf height by 1U to free up some space as well. Our checks
indicate that both the in-rack power shelf and 800VDC power rack solutions are ongoing
now, and we believe that rack space, power redundancy and system reliability are all the
factors being considered. We'll closely monitor developments on this front.
Exhibit 22: Liquid cooled power shelf
Source: Megmeet
Exhibit 23: Liquid cooled power shelf in rack
Source: Megmeet

Solid state transformers on the Nvidia RVL for the first time
In the MGX ecosystem board, where Nvidia usually list the recommenced vendor lists and
product samples, we saw SST this time. Delta, Heron. DG Matrax, Sungrow, Solaredge,
Megmeet, Luxshare, etc.

### Page 12

M
Idea
12
Exhibit 24: SST vendors on the Nvidia MGX ecosystem board
Source: Morgan Stanley
Looking further ahead, Solid-State Transformers (SSTs) represent a potential next-
generation evolution of power infrastructure. Unlike traditional transformers that operate
primarily as passive devices, SSTs leverage power electronics to provide voltage
conversion, power conditioning, and bidirectional energy management within a single
platform. This enables greater controllability and flexibility while supporting increasingly

digitalized power networks.
Although SSTs remain at an early stage of commercialization and face challenges related
to economics, reliability, and large-scale deployment, the technology aligns well with long-
term data center trends. As AI campuses evolve into utility-scale power consumers
incorporating energy storage, distributed generation, and advanced power management,
SSTs could eventually become a key enabling technology.

### Page 13

M
Idea
Morgan Stanley Research 13
Therefore, while HVDC is expected to drive the next phase of power architecture
upgrades, SSTs represent a longer-term technology roadmap that could further transform
how future data centers distribute and manage electricity.

architecture

2030, as the technology begins to replace a portion of the >$15bn conventional
transformer market, particularly in smaller-power applications. While SST penetration is
expected to remain relatively modest versus the overall transformer market, the
opportunity is significant for relevant players in the longer term.

Valuation Methodology and Risks
Lite-On Technology (2301.TW)
Base case: P/E multiple of 17x based on average 2027-28 EPS. We believe our target multiple
is justified by the 47% earnings CAGR we estimate for 2025-28, implying a PEG of 0.4, versus
the stock's 16x P/E and 2% earnings CAGR during 2022-25, which implied a PEG of 7.2.
Risks to Upside
n Stronger optical business growth
n Stronger data center/server demand
n PC/NB demand growth

n Downtrend in raw material prices
Risks to Downside
n Macro demand weakness
n Weaker data center/server demand
n PC/NB demand slowdown
n Uptrend in raw material prices
Delta Electronics Inc. (2308.TW)
Base case: P/E multiple of 30x based on average 2027-28 EPS.                          We believe our target multiple
is justified by the 63% earnings CAGR we estimate for 2025-28, implying a PEG of 0.5, versus
the stock's ~23x P/E and 23% earnings CAGR during 2022-25, which implied a PEG of 1.0.

Risks to Upside
n Faster-than-expected global economic growth
n Falling raw material prices
n Margin improvement
Risks to Downside
n Global economic slowdown
n Ongoing component supply tightness
n Rising raw material prices
n Margin contraction as a result of higher operating expenses or faster ASP erosion
Chroma Ate Inc. (2360.TW)
Base case, 45x 2027e P/E. We use P/E as our valuation methodology because we think it better
captures Chroma's earnings growth momentum. The stock traded at an average valuation of

~19x P/E during 2014-25 with an operating profit CAGR of 20%, implying a PEG of 1.0. We think
our target multiple of 45x is justified by the 61% operating profit CAGR we project for
2025-28.

n Better-than-expected macro conditions
n Stronger semi and photonics and power testing demand

## Document 107: 261019_citi_dynapack

### Page 1

Improving 3Q GPM helped by better mix — Dynapack's preliminary 3Q26 results 
showed margin recovery yet weak PC sales remained the key drag on the earnings. 
3Q26 EPS of NT$2.33 (+72% q/q and +49% y/y) missed our/BBG est by 45%/27%, 
although we believe the earnings shortfall was well anticipated given the 3Q sales 
miss owing to the delayed shipment of one of the BBU products and weaker PC 
demand. GPM improved q/q on a richer product mix, with higher-margin BBU 
making up c.50% of 3Q sales on our estimate, while OPM recovered to 10.7%, 
+4.2ppt q/q and +1.3ppt y/y. Nevertheless, the sales shortfall constrained op 
leverage, leaving margin improvement insufficient to offset weaker sales. 
PSU vendor shortages weigh on Oct; 12kW ramp to drive Nov sales rebound — 
Our industry checks suggest that Dynapack's major PSU vendor has encountered 
component supply shortages (related to power management IC) and therefore 
delayed BBU pull-ins, weighing on shipments of Dynapack's existing 3kW and 5kW 
BBU products in Oct. We therefore expect Oct-26 BBU sales to decline slightly m/m. 
Meanwhile, PC sales should recover sequentially and more than to offset the BBU 
softness, supporting a slight m/m increase in group sales. Looking beyond Oct-26, 
we expect accelerating shipments of the new 12kW BBU series from Nov-26 to drive 
a strong rebound in BBU sales.

Implications — We reiterate our bullish stance on the BBU sector despite near-term
volatility from external supply-chain constraints, which is likely to put pressure on
near-term sentiment. We view the component-driven delays in product pull-ins
primarily as a shipment-timing issue, with underlying demand remaining strong into
2027E. We also see ample opportunity for Dynapack to gain market share from

Panasonic, its major Japanese peer, particularly as power architectures migrate
toward HVDC. This share-gain potential remains intact, reinforcing our confidence
in Dynapack's medium term BBU growth outlook.

Dynapack International (3211.TWO)
19 October 2026 Citi Research

Dynapack International
Valuation
We assign a 32x target P/E to our 2027E EPS of NT$25 to derive a target price of NT$800. We value Dynapack based on 2027E
target P/E as we believe 2027E better reflects its earnings power after BBU capacity expansion. Our target P/E of 32x is based
on our forecast EPS CAGR of 66% in 2025-2027E and a PEG of 0.5x, which we benchmark against its major PSU partner, as we

expect Dynapack to grow alongside its partner.
Risks
Our quantitative model assigns a High Risk rating to Dynapack. However, we believe such a rating is not warranted for
Dynapack due to: 1) the company is generating solid revenue and earnings growth over next 3 years; the underlying industry,
BBU for AIDC, also enjoys secular growth; 2) the business model that involves with product customization and strict
safety/reliability requirements would imply relatively benign competition and therefore stable pricing and margins; and 3) the

company has a strong balance sheet and has been in net cash position since 2019.
Key downside risks to our price target/rating are: 1) Slower-than-expected BBU adoption in AIDC, 2) Delay in PSU customer
qualifications, 3) Competition from larger battery or power solution vendors, 4) Safety, certification and reliability issues.
Panasonic Holdings
(6752.T; ¥3957.0; 1; 16 Oct 26; 15:30)
Valuation
To derive our target price, we use a global sum-of-the-parts (SoTP) approach. In the case of Panasonic Holdings, we reference

52 other companies in the Citi coverage universe to set a fair value PER for each business division, which we sum to derive our
overall target price.
Panasonic fair-value PERs for individual business segments range from 8x to 31x, and have a weighted average of 26.2x
(excluding subsidies in the battery business), taking minority interest and equity in earnings into account. We estimate
business-derived equity value of ¥5,000/share. We calculate the present value of the amount we expect Panasonic to receive

in battery subsidies over five years at ¥400/share. Combining these two values, we derive a target price of ¥5,400.
Risks
Risks to our target price include 1) unsuccessful management policies or strategies resulting in weaker-than-expected
business investment returns or sales growth, or conversely producing greater-than-expected results at a relatively early stage,
2) stagnation in the global economy or an upturn in it, and rises or declines in demand in the electrical industry, and 3) forex

trends. If these factors manifest themselves differently than we have anticipated, the share price may vary from our target
price.

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.

Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment

rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating

and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price

chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the

expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.
Catalyst Watch/Short Term Views (“STV”) Ratings Disclosure:

Catalyst Watch and STV Upside/Downside calls: Citi Research may also include a Catalyst Watch or STV Upside or Downside
call to indicate the analyst expects the share price to rise (fall) in absolute terms over a specified period of 30 or 90 days in
reaction to one or more specific near-term catalysts or events impacting the company or the market. A Catalyst Watch will be
published when Analyst confidence is high that an impact to share price will occur; it will be a STV when confidence level is

moderate. A Catalyst Watch or STV Upside/Downside call will automatically expire at the end of the specified 30/90 day
period. The Catalyst Watch will also be automatically removed if share price performance (calculated at market close) exceeds
15% against the direction of the call (unless over-ridden by the analyst). The analyst may also remove a Catalyst Watch or STV
call prior to the end of the specified period in a published research note. A Catalyst Watch/STV Upside or Downside call may be

our ratings distribution table for our Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside rating system. However, we reiterate that we do
not consider No View to be a recommendation. For all Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside calls, risk exists that the
catalyst(s) and associated share-price movement will not materialize as expected.

Economics | 3

### Page 4

Monthly Economics
Sector Analysis
Personal Consumption Expenditures (PCE)
• We've lifted our Q3 expectation for real PCE on incoming data and a slightly firmer consumer
backdrop in the wake of recent revisions.
Consumer spending looks firmer in the third quarter, and we now expect real PCE to rise at a 3.4%
annualized clip. This upward revision reflects recent data, with August consumption coming in firm
with real spending rising by the fastest monthly clip in nearly two years, but also reflects a slightly

firmer consumer backdrop in the wake of the BEA's annual revisions released late September. Although
revisions did not materially alter spending, they lifted the level of household income in recent years,
which also boosted the personal saving rate. The trajectory of real income growth remains weak, but
the consumer still looks to be in a slightly stronger financial position than previously believed, which
helps explain recent resilience in spending and suggests consumers may have a bit more runway ahead.

Investment: Equipment, Intellectual Property Products and Inventories
• The AI build out will lead to another surge in business fixed investment spending in Q3, though the
effect on headline growth will be partly offset by stronger imports.
Business fixed investment spending continues to be supported primarily by the high-tech buildout.
We forecast real equipment investment to rise at around a 16% annualized clip in Q3, but part of
this boost to headline GDP growth will be offset by stronger import growth that is facilitating the

build out. Net imports of computers are tracking to surge in the third quarter, which will provide a
sizable lift to information processing equipment investment, or around half of overall equipment.
And while non-defense capital goods shipments (including aircraft) slipped in August, the near-term
growth trajectory remains supportive of a pickup in equipment. The rebuild in inventories we have
been anticipating is also tracking to be a bit stronger as businesses replenish drawn down product.

Investment: Residential
• The abrupt rise in mortgage rates is likely to dampen residential investment over the next few
quarters. Residential spending should find support next year as longer-term interest rates
moderate somewhat.
We are unlikely to see another positive residential investment reading in Q3. Rising mortgage rates

have sent a chill over builder outlooks, pushing sales expectations to their weakest level in more than
three years. While builders have responded with deeper price cuts, affordability pressures continue
to weigh on single-family sales and construction outlays. Apartment construction is simultaneously
soft amid rising financing costs and modest rent growth, and home improvement spending remains
constrained by high materials costs and interest rates. While residential investment is likely to remain

4 | Economics

### Page 5

Economic Outlook: October 2026 Economics
a drag this year, we expect a slight moderation in longer-term interest rates in 2027, which would
provide a more supportive backdrop.
Investment: Nonresidential Structures
• We have not made material changes to our nonresidential structures forecast. Despite elevated
interest rates, data center and related spending should continue to support structures investment
through our forecast horizon.
Nonresidential structures spending has accelerated sharply, overwhelmingly driven by data centers

and electric power facilities. As data center construction expands geographically and demand rises
for semiconductors, high-tech capex has also started to generate positive spillovers for warehousing
and manufacturing construction. This buildout, which appears relatively insensitive to interest rates,
should be enough to sustain solid growth in structures investment even as spending across most
other categories remains essentially flat. Domestic energy is another green shoot, with elevated prices

supporting ongoing growth in active rotary rigs.

Net Exports & Trade
• An expected drag from trade in Q3 got larger in light of a near-record trade deficit in August, net
exports is now expected to lop off three full percentage points from third quarter growth.

Exports increased in August for the first time in four months, but the increase was more than offset by
an even larger increase in imports. The upshot was a widening in the trade deficit to a near-record gap
of more than $105 billion. Going into the report, we had a 1.9 percentage point drag from net exports
penciled in for Q3 GDP; we revised that to a full 3.0 percentage points drag in light of the record gap.
Even a modest narrowing in the final months of the year would likely mean a lift to headline GDP in

Q4, but the real story here is that trade will remain a headwind throughout the forecast period owing
largely to surging imports of tech-related products amid the AI boom.
Labor Market
• The jobs market remains stable. We continue to expect the unemployment rate to end the year at
4.2% and hold near that level in 2027.
We modestly revised down our near-term payroll growth forecast following the September jobs
report, but otherwise we have left our labor market outlook unchanged. Measures of labor demand

growth continue to point to a stable labor market. Job openings have inched higher on a year-ago
basis but remain broadly steady while initial jobless claims, WARN notices and Challenger job cut
announcements continue to signal low layoffs. Labor force participation appears to be improving after
its sharp pullback in the first half of the year. Looking ahead, we expect payroll growth to average
around 60K per month in 2027, roughly in line with our estimate of breakeven job growth. That should

Economics | 5

### Page 6

Monthly Economics
keep the unemployment rate near 4.2% in 2027 and leave labor market conditions broadly consistent
with "full employment." Notably, our expectation for labor cost growth of ~3.4% is the reason we
continue to reiterate that labor cost growth is not a contributor to the excess inflation seen in the
data.
Inflation
• We expect the core PCE deflator to rise 3.2% and 2.6% Q4/Q4 in 2026 and 2027, respectively.
Headline inflation remains heavily influenced by developments in the Middle East, with higher oil

prices lifting gasoline and diesel prices. Food inflation has been tame this year but looks poised to
strengthen as higher transportation and commodity prices work their way through supply chains.
Beyond food and energy, price pressures remain concentrated in a handful of categories, most notably
airfares and AI-related tech goods. Core goods inflation is showing signs of stabilizing, as waning tariff-
related price pressures have been offset by resilient demand tied to the AI buildout. We expect some

improvement here in 2027. Still-low shelter inflation and cooler price growth for airfares and health
care should help bring about some moderation in core services inflation next year. We expect the core
PCE deflator to rise 3.2% on a Q4-over-Q4 basis in 2026 before slowing to 2.6% in 2027.

Fiscal Policy
• Fiscal year (FY) 2026 ended on September 30, and the federal government tallied a $2.0 trillion
budget deficit (6.5% of GDP). We project the deficit to be unchanged in dollar terms in FY 2027

and a bit smaller as a share of GDP (~6%).
• Another continuing resolution to extend government funding past the December 11 deadline
seems like the most likely outcome at this time for the FY 2027 budget. It is possible Republicans
use the lame duck session of Congress to increase defense spending and raise the debt ceiling.
Looking ahead to the lame duck session of Congress that will follow the midterm elections, Congress
needs to pass a bill that extends government funding past December 11 to avert a government

shutdown. Another continuing resolution that punts the issue into Q1 seems like the most likely
outcome. Perhaps more interesting will be whether Republicans attempt to use budget reconciliation
to pass one more partisan piece of legislation. If they do, we suspect the two things at the top the
agenda will be more funding for national defense and immigration enforcement priorities as well as a
debt ceiling increase. For the latter, raising it in the lame duck would remove some leverage Democrats

could have in 2027 if they recapture one or both chambers. It is still very early, but we estimate the
debt ceiling "X date" by which Congress must act or face default is sometime in late Q3/early Q4 of
next year.

Economic Outlook: October 2026 Economics
Monetary Policy & Interest Rates
• Our base case forecast is for the FOMC to hike the fed funds rate by 25 bps in December and
then keep the policy rate unchanged in 2027 at 4.00%-4.25%, with risks skewed toward additional
tightening.
As we go to print, the FOMC seems more likely than not to keep the fed funds rate unchanged in
October. But given the rationale under which the Fed started this tightening cycle and the forcefulness

with which Warsh has driven home his displeasure with elevated inflation, it seems entirely plausible
to us that October will be a live meeting. At a minimum, the recent inflation data seem to make a hike
in December a lock. Past December, the outlook will be highly dependent on future inflation data. Our
expectation is that core inflation will cool gradually in 2027, and we still do not see signs of demand-
side inflation in the data. But hiking cycles have an inertial component to them, and the recent inflation

data reset the clock to zero on the Fed seeing several good inflation prints in a row. This creates upside
risk to our base case for the fed funds rate.

International Developments
• Global growth remains resilient, leaving our 2026 and 2027 forecasts unchanged. Our inflation
forecasts are also unchanged, although risks of delayed price pass-through could prolong inflation
pressures into 2027.

• Monetary policy continues to lean toward tightening across advanced economies, while emerging
market central banks are increasingly cautious, either holding rates or easing more gradually as
inflation pressures rebuild.
The balance of risks to global inflation remains tilted to the upside, complicating the monetary policy
outlook even as growth has proven more resilient than expected. Elevated energy prices, particularly
for refined petroleum products, persistent geopolitical uncertainty and El Niño-related food price

risks could prolong inflationary pressures into 2027. While second-round effects still remain relatively
contained, repeated supply shocks are testing central banks’ willingness to look through the temporary
price increases, particularly where inflation expectations remain elevated, even as higher government
bond yields are already contributing to tighter financial conditions.
Across advanced economies, central banks face increasingly difficult trade-offs as persistent inflation

pressures limit the scope for policy accommodation. The Eurozone remains particularly vulnerable
to energy supply disruptions, with elevated natural gas prices and low storage levels adding to
inflation risks. We continue to expect a 25 bps European Central Bank hike in December, followed by a
prolonged hold through 2027. Fiscal concerns are also adding to pressure, particularly in France, where
the selloff in government debt has pushed yields higher. In the U.K., resilient activity and renewed

Economics | 7

### Page 8

Monthly Economics
inflation pressures similarly underpin our expectation for a Bank of England hike in November,
followed by a hold through 2027.
Elsewhere, policy paths are becoming more differentiated. After four hikes this year brought the Cash
Rate to 4.60%, we expect the Reserve Bank of Australia to remain on hold, although renewed inflation
pressures or stronger-than-expected activity could prompt further tightening. We also still expect
the Bank of Canada to hold at 2.25%, with subdued growth weighing against energy-driven inflation

risks, although incoming inflation data over the coming weeks will be critical in determining whether
persistent price pressures warrant tightening monetary policy perhaps as early as the December
meeting. Japan remains an exception to the broader hold outlook: while the yen's recovery provides
some relief from imported inflation, elevated energy prices and import dependence continue to pose
upside risks. We expect the Bank of Japan to hike again by year-end and now expect an additional 25

bps hike in Q3-2027, bringing the terminal rate to 1.75%.
The emerging market story has increasingly shifted from easing toward patience or selective
tightening. In Asia, China's weak domestic demand and persistent property-sector strains continue to
warrant policy support, with AI-related exports providing some offset. We expect a more gradual pace
of Reserve Requirement Ratio cuts alongside targeted monetary and fiscal measures. India faces the

opposite challenge, with elevated food and energy prices and rupee depreciation strengthening the
case for further Reserve Bank of India tightening. In Latin America, peso depreciation and elevated
energy costs pose upside inflation risks for Mexico, although limited exchange-rate pass-through
should allow Banxico to remain on hold while policymakers monitor underlying inflation and inflation
expectations. In Brazil, election-related uncertainty and renewed inflation pressures are likely to delay

further easing, with cuts expected to resume in Q1-2027 once policymakers see more convincing
evidence of disinflation.

## Document 258: El Niño 2026–2027_ multiple pressures on prices and supplies (2026-09-30)

### Page 1

30 September 2026
The bank
for a changing
world
EL NIÑO 2026–2027: MULTIPLE PRESSURES ON PRICES AND SUPPLIES
Hélène Drouot
The 2026–2027 El Niño climate event is expected to be one of the most intense in the last fifty years, with sea-surface-temperature
anomalies potentially exceeding +3°C in the equatorial Pacific. According to the World Meteorological Organisation (WMO), El Niño
is expected to persist until February 2027, with asymmetrical effects, as South-East Asia and northern Latin America will experience

droughts, while southern Brazil and Argentina could see heavy rainfall. Above all, El Niño is compounding the effects of global warming
and exacerbating existing extreme-weather events (droughts, floods and heatwaves). The economic effects of this climate shock mainly
play out through three avenues: agricultural commodity prices, energy and logistics (see heatmap).
Agricultural commodity prices: volatility is causing tensions, with inflationary effects

In India and South-East Asia, droughts will affect harvests, pushing up prices (particularly for coffee, cocoa, rice and palm oil). Conversely, in
southern Brazil and Argentina, heavy rainfall will boost soya and maize production, putting downward pressure on prices. According to the IMF,
El Niño causes global food prices to rise by between 5% (in a typical episode) and 8% (very high intensity) year-on-year, with the full impact being
felt 6 to 12 months after the climatic peak.

Therefore, for a given country, the weighting of food in its inflation index (CPI) plays a decisive role. As a result, all other things being equal, an 8%
rise in prices adds around 1 pp to inflation when food accounts for 13% of the CPI basket (average for countries shown in green on the heatmap),
1.8 pp when this share reaches 22% (yellow), 2.8 pp at 35% (orange) and nearly 4 pp above 49% (red).
The risks are skewed to the upside, as the 8% estimate does not take into account additional losses linked to droughts, floods and other extre -

me events, while policy responses to shocks (stockpiling by importers and increased export restrictions) may amplify and prolong price pres -
sures. The rice market between 2023 and 2024 illustrates this well, as the combination of rice-export restrictions imposed by India in July 2023
(in order to protect its domestic market) and of the drought in South-East Asia contributed to a 30% average increase in the price of rice over the
course of 2023 and kept global prices high for several months after the end of the El Niño episode.

Energy production sector: heavy reliance on hydroelectricity leaves the sector vulnerable to a second supply shock
Droughts will reduce hydroelectric power generation in Latin America and South-East Asia. As a result, some countries will have to turn to other,
more expensive solutions, such as fossil fuels, against a backdrop of already high oil and gas prices. This shift will increase global demand for
oil and put pressure on prices which, according to the IMF, could persist for up to four quarters after the initial El Niño shock. For example,

in Colombia, where hydropower accounts for 55% of electricity generation, a 30% drop in output (already seen in 2023–2024) could increase the
cost of electricity generation by 15 to 20% (assuming that the cost of thermal power generation is twice as high as hydropower). In Ecuador (where
hydropower accounts for 70% of electricity generation) and Vietnam (30%), electricity shortages are likely to disrupt local industries.
SOURCE: IMF, WORLD BANK, EMBER, BNP PARIBAS CALCULATIONS

Vulnerabilities differ, depending on a country’s dependence on food, energy and logistics

Reading note: Scores are standardised on a scale of 0 to 10 for ease of comparison between countries and supply channels.
- Food vulnerability: based on the weight of food in the CPI.
- Energy vulnerability: based on the share of hydropower in electricity generation.
- Logistics vulnerability: the ‘Trade and transport-related infrastructure’ component of the World Bank’s Logistics Perfor-

mance Index, which measures the quality of the infrastructure required for transport and international trade.

Chart of the Week / 30 September 2026 economic-research.bnpparibas.com
Logistics: transport infrastructure is another point of vulnerability
The final channel relates to disruptions to freight transport against a backdrop of already weakened value chains. The variable used in our
heatmap is a proxy for economies’ abilities to absorb a shock (particularly a climate-related one) affecting their transport infrastructure (for

example, by maintaining traffic flow, diverting or reorganising transport flows). The heatmap highlights the particularly high vulnerability
of Latin America. Its capacity to absorb a logistics shock is relatively limited, and there is significant exposure at a strategic chokepoint, the
Panama Canal, through which approximately 5% of global trade passes. Low rainfall in the canal basin has already led to the number of
daily transits being reduced in September 2026 (as in 2023–2024); further restrictions are expected in October (to 29 transits per day, down

from 36 in August), which will increase transport times and costs. Europe is also being affected, with low water levels on the Rhine and the
Danube already disrupting river transport and electricity generation.
Latin America and South-East Asia are hit hardest
Therefore, the economies most at risk are not necessarily those that will suffer the greatest climate shock, but those where multiple trans -
mission channels converge, including a high weighting of food prices in inflation, reliance on hydroelectricity, fragile transport infrastructure

and limited fiscal room for manoeuvre. Latin America and South-East Asia are home to some of the most vulnerable countries, particularly
due to their dependence on food prices and hydroelectricity. Colombia and Ecuador are noteworthy for their high exposure to energy risk,
while Ghana, India and the Philippines are some of the economies most vulnerable to a food shock. Latin America faces a combination of
vulnerabilities, such as high exposure to food and energy price shocks in some countries, which are compounded by logistics risks, as currently

illustrated by the restrictions on traffic through the Panama Canal.
helene.drouot@bnpparibas.com

## Document 259: Energy shock_ Dashboard 2026 vs. 2022 - October 2026 (2026-10-02)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Energy shock_ Dashboard 2026 vs. 2022 - October 2026 (2026-10-02).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-10-02
Pages: 1
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Energy shock: Dashboard 2026 vs. 2022
10/2/2026 2
Will the same causes produce the same effects? In other words, will the war in Iran and the resulting
surge in oil and gas prices lead to an inflationary shock comparable to that seen in 2022? Will their
negative effects on growth be the same as those of the war in Ukraine and the subsequent energy
shock?
We have selected a set of indicators to track the impact of this new energy shock — caused by the war

in the Middle East — on activity and prices in the Eurozone, the United States, oil and gas markets and
emerging countries, and to see how much the current situation resembles that of 2022 at the outbreak
of the conflict in Ukraine.
This dashboard features charts and comments that will be updated on a monthly basis for as long as
necessary.
Overall, based on data available through September 2026, economic activity continues to show
resilience. The high levels of business climate surveys illustrate this well. Household confidence,

however, is more vulnerable and sensitive to energy price pressures. Financing conditions in emerging
economies also continue to hold up well against the shock (much better than in 2022) as well as
against the tensions in DM bond markets, which intensified this summer, partly in the wake of the
reinforcing upward pressure on energy prices. Higher energy prices are also fueling inflationary
pressures and pushing inflation even higher. In particular, Eurozone inflation rose by 0.6 percentage

points to 3.8% year-on-year (September flash estimate), reaching its highest level in three years.

to a 3-year high

Despite hot inﬂation prints in September and the Fed having
signalled that further action to tame inﬂation will likely be
needed, markets and economists are united in expecting the
central bank to leave interest rates on hold at the 28 October
FOMC meeting. Ongoing concerns about elevated price

James Knightley, Padhraic Garvey, CFA and Chris Turner
The Fed looks set to hold rates steady at the October FOMC meeting before
hiking again in December
Fed ofﬁcials play for time
The immediate reaction to the surprise September 0.3% month-on-
month core CPI print was to send the chances of an October rate
hike above 50%. But this didn’t last long. Earlier comments from
Federal Reserve Vice Chairs Philip Jeﬀerson and Michelle Bowman
and Governor Chris Waller, signalling no rush to decide on the next
policy move, quickly calmed markets. The fact that Fed Chair Kevin
Warsh subsequently chose not to talk up the chances of action
when he spoke at the IMF meetings in Bangkok, unlike at the

Jackson Hole Conference before the September hike, further

While it is true to say that recent jobs numbers have been
disappointing and interest rate-sensitive sectors, such as the
housing market, are experiencing increasing pain, the economy is
growing robustly, and inﬂation remains above target. Fed oﬃcials
themselves have made it clear that they think more tightening of
monetary policy is going to be required to bring inﬂation to heel.

Given that the latest Federal Reserve Summary of Economic
Projections showed four FOMC members projecting 50bp of rate
hikes between now and the end of the year, the decision is unlikely
to be unanimous. Beth Hammack, Neel Kashkari and Lorie Logan
all voted to hike in July when the rest of the committee wanted to
hold steady. All three could again dissent in favour of hiking.
The press release is likely to be brief, and we also expect another
short and snappy press conference from Chair Warsh. He's likely to

keep things quite matter-of-fact given his lack of enthusiasm for
forward guidance. We expect his message to be that the economy
remains in decent shape, unemployment is low, and inﬂation is
above target. The policy changes being made will be determined
by the trends in incoming data, with oﬃcials currently thinking that
they probably have more work to do.

Looking towards December
We will have two jobs reports and one inﬂation print ahead of the
December decision. The November CPI will be released the day after
the Fed meeting, and oﬃcials will almost certainly get to see it. As
such, they will have more information than the market does,

meaning a surprise can’t be completely ruled out.
We are forecasting a 25bp rate hike at the December meeting
(leaving the Fed funds target rate range at 4-4.25%) followed by
a prolonged pause through to December 2027, when we have that
December 2026 hike being reversed. This is in line with the current
consensus forecast, while the Fed’s own projections (its central
forecast) see one hike this year with rates left unchanged
throughout 2027.
While the sharp move higher in Treasury yields and the ﬁrmer

dollar are tightening ﬁnancial conditions to a degree, our forecast
path for the Fed assumes that in the couple of months following
the midterm elections we see an agreement that improves the ﬂow
of energy from the Persian Gulf, but signiﬁcantly, also allows for a
sharp reduction in shipping and insurance costs. It's the latter point
that is crucial to delivering lower pump prices at a time when
reﬁneries are working at capacity. If correct, this should

meaningfully ease inﬂation pressures, and with US wage growth
(the biggest cost input for corporate America) looking very benign
at just 3% and the largest component of the inﬂation basket –
housing at 35% by weight – set to experience more disinﬂation, we

The risk to our view is clearly tilted towards a longer-lasting conﬂict
rather than one that is resolved imminently. This means we would
not rule out additional hike(s) in 1Q 2027, but with households
experiencing a greater squeeze on spending power (weak wage
growth amid rising energy costs) and bond yields likely pushing
even higher – leading to new cycle highs for mortgage rates and

corporate borrowing costs – the risks of a weaker economy will
increase. As such, in this more stressed economic scenario,
eventual cuts to policy rates will likely be swifter and more
substantial in 2H 2027 than in our base case.
The Fed to leave well enough alone and let
Treasuries do their own thing
A solid performance for US Treasuries in the past week has acted to
reduce the prior tightening impulse that had gripped the market.
The 10yr yield below 5% obviously contrasts dramatically with the

prior break above 5.25%. We’re not convinced the more recent
downward lurch is persisting. But its genesis lends itself to a softer
approach from the FOMC at this meeting.
The risk-oﬀ tone creates a heightened risk proﬁle for the US
economy, raising the probability that the Fed holds at this meeting.
The battering that the French bond market has been taking is
another important impulse, as it heightens the broader sense of
bond angst. And at the margin, it can add to systemic pressure.

This is not the type of environment where the Fed would be inclined
to add to concerns by tightening policy. It’s the perfect meeting to
keep rates on hold.
Whether we remain below 5% for the 10yr yield is a whole other
question. We're minded to expect it to snap back above, as we still
have inﬂation, ﬁscal deﬁcit, and AI-boom impulses at play. The Fed
will no doubt remind us of these ongoing pressures. Plus, a break
back above 5% will likely need a degree of calm in the risk space

ﬁrst. We think it should ultimately move back above 5%; the timing,

Warsh FOMC meetings have set the dollar
tone since June
The dollar should be able to hold on to most of its September
gains, as Fed communication is likely to remain supportive of
higher US yields. If the dollar is to correct lower, a renewed tech-led
risk-oﬀ move rather than a dovish shift in Fed messaging is the
more likely catalyst.
After a sharp 4% run higher through September and into early
October, the dollar has entered a more consolidative phase. Rate

diﬀerentials are the most important driver at the moment, and
these have moved slightly against the dollar this month as the US
tech sell-oﬀ has pared back Fed tightening expectations.
Broadening out the lens, it looks like Warsh-led FOMC meetings
have set the four-to-six-week trend for the dollar this year. June’s
meeting was hawkish, July’s was seen as dovish, and the Fed
delivered a hawkish hike in September.
We suspect that Warsh has learned from the criticism he received

over July's FOMC meeting, where he implied that the bond market
was doing the tightening (read as the Fed would not have to). That
probably means he will not be drawn into concluding that wider
credit spreads and softer equity prices mean tighter ﬁnancial
conditions now require fewer Fed hikes.
Assuming that Warsh sticks to the script that monetary policy
credibility remains the Fed’s top priority, short-dated US yields can
remain well-supported, and the dollar can largely hold on to the

gains it has made since early September. And the euro looks like it

2027 is likely to be another eventful year for European utilities.
Geopolitical uncertainty, volatility in energy markets and
signiﬁcant investment pipelines, together with the associated

Corporate hybrids remain an important part
of the funding mix
Utilities have raised €19bn with corporate hybrids so far in 2026,
the highest level since 2015. Hybrid issuance helps protect the
credit proﬁles of these companies as they ﬁnance their extensive

investment pipelines with debt alongside cash ﬂows.

High capital expenditure drives supply
Alongside redemptions, bond supply continues to be driven by
large capital expenditure. We expect combined investment to top
€200bn next year for large European utility issuers. German names

have the highest investment needs for 2027, up around 30% year-
on-year, while Italian, UK, and Dutch names are also among those
seeing a clear increase.
The energy transition continues to drive signiﬁcant investment
needs, with growing electriﬁcation and renewable generation
requiring substantial expansion and reinforcement of transmission
and distribution grids. Regulated utilities are particularly impacted
by higher grid investments, but integrated utilities are also likely to

see higher capital expenditure next year.
Higher interest rates may make some companies more selective in
their investments. The bulk of these programmes is likely to
proceed, however, as the acute investment needs of many network
utilities are unlikely to change any time soon. Potential execution

We expect the September quarter inflation report (out
on 22 October) will show that New Zealand consumer
prices rose 0.7% over the past three months. That would
see the annual inflation rate slipping back to 3.7%, down
from 4.1% in the year to June. However, as we discuss

in our recent forecast update, that’s likely to be just a
temporary reprieve with the recent rise in oil prices set to
push inflation up again over the coming months.
16 Oct 2026 | Satish Ranchhod, Senior Economist | +64 9 336 5668 | +64 21 710 852 | satish.ranchhod@westpac.co.nz
ECONOMIC BULLETIN
CPI preview, September quarter 2026 –
Thursday 22 October, 10:45am.

### Page 2

2
Westpac New Zealand Economics
Key drivers of quarterly inflation.
Underpinning our forecast for 0.7% inflation in the
September quarter are some big price movements in
both directions.
The largest move in prices this quarter has actually been
a sharp fall in fuel costs. Fuel prices aren’t low by any
stretch of the imagination. However, average prices for
both petrol and diesel over the September quarter were
lower than in the June quarter when prices rocketed

higher in the wake of the Middle East conflict – see the
chart below. In terms of specifics, the average price for 91
unleaded was around 6% lower in September than in the
June quarter, while diesel prices were close to 16% lower.
Fuel spending accounts for around 4% of the CPI, and
these moves have taken 0.4ppts off inflation this quarter.

While prices at the pump weren’t as high as they were in
the June quarter, fuel costs are still painfully high. And
that’s been bleeding through into higher prices for other
goods and services. Of note, the September quarter
saw large increases in passenger transport costs, with
international airfares up 14% and domestic airfares up

nearly 8%. Together those prices account for around 2%
of the CPI.
We also expect building costs (10% of the CPI) will rise by
1.6% this quarter following a similarly chunky rise in June.
Again, much of that rise has been related to higher global
oil prices which have been flowing through to higher
operating and material costs. That’s occurred at the same
time as the amount of planned construction work has
been rising.
The September quarter also sees the annual increases

in local council rates and charges for services like water
supply (around 4% of the CPI). We expect these costs will
be up around 7.4% this year, continuing the run of large
increases seen in previous years.
Lastly, the past few months saw a 1.0% rise in food prices
(18% of the CPI), largely as a result of the seasonal rise in
the price for fresh vegetables.
On the downside, we’ve seen continued softness in
housing rents, which are the largest component of

the CPI, accounting for 10% of the basket. With low
population growth and ample supply, rents have
effectively been flat since the start of this year. In annual
terms, rental inflation is the lowest it’s been in more
than two decades, with regions like Wellington seeing
outright falls.

Where could we be surprised?
There are some key areas where we see the risk
of a downside surprise. The first is the price for
communications equipment, like mobile phones. After
a large rise in the June quarter, we could see a sizeable

drop in prices this quarter.
There could also be softness in used car prices, especially
with high fuel prices encouraging a shift away from
combustion engine vehicles.
However, the risks aren’t all to one side. Demand for more
environmentally friendly new cars has picked up, and that
could push up prices for those vehicles. In addition, we’re
watching to see if high fuel costs are spilling over into
other prices as we’re seeing in sectors like construction.

Core inflation around 3%, not easing.
With big swings in fuel costs and concerns that this is
spilling over into a broader lift in inflation pressures,
the RBNZ will be paying close attention to the various
measures of core inflation (core inflation measures
smooth through quarter-to-quarter swings in individual
prices and instead track the underlying trend in inflation).
We expect that core inflation measures will point to
continued firmness in underlying price pressures, with

several closely watched measures likely to take a step
higher this quarter. In terms of specifics, we expect:
• CPI ex-fuel inflation: +3.0% yr (vs +2.9% previously)
• CPI ex-fuel and food: +3.2% yr (vs +2.9% previously)
• CPI ex-fuel, food and household energy: +2.9% yr (vs
+2.5% previously)

### Page 3

3
Westpac New Zealand Economics
We’ll also be watching the distribution of price
changes to see if there’s been a material increase in
the number of prices that are increasing. That could
be an early indication that businesses, even those
with a limited exposure to fuel costs, are starting to
factor in expectations of continued high inflation when
setting prices.
As we’ve noted before, the various measures of core
inflation were not low even before the recent fuel shock.

That means the RBNZ has limited headroom to absorb
upside inflation surprises.
Much of that firmness in core inflation has been due
to increases in domestically oriented non-tradables
prices. We estimate these prices rose by 1.3% over the
September quarter. That would see annual non-tradables
inflation rising to 3.6%, up from 3.4% last quarter.
Non-tradables inflation is a key focus for the RBNZ and
it’s been running at these above-average levels for around

a year now. That’s despite the softness in economic
growth and the labour market. In part, this firmness in
domestic inflation is due to continued large increases in
administered prices, like council rates.
On the imported front, lower fuel costs are expected to
see tradables prices falling by 0.2% over the quarter.
That would result in annual tradables inflation dropping
back to 3.9% from 4.9% previously. Excluding food and
fuel costs, we estimate that tradables prices were up

2.2% over the past year (vs 1.9% in the year to June). That
would be the highest level in three years with the fall in
the NZD boosting import costs.

How do our forecasts compare to the
RBNZ’s assumptions?
The RBNZ’s last published forecast from their September

policy statement assumed that consumer prices would
rise by 0.8%qtr (3.9%yr). That’s slightly higher than we’re
expecting. Most of the difference is because the RBNZ is
expecting higher imported inflation.
A result in line with our forecast would give the RBNZ a
little more breathing room ahead of their upcoming policy
review on 28 October. We’re expecting they will leave the
OCR on hold at this meeting, while markets are pricing in
a roughly 66% chance of a 25bp hike.

However, even if the overall inflation result is close to
forecast, the underlying detail will be important. The
RBNZ will be watching closely for signs that high fuel
prices are spilling over into other prices, especially with
global oil prices taking another step higher recently. With
that in mind, the strength of the various core inflation
measures will be a key focus for the RBNZ.
Furthermore, while annual inflation is expected to
soften in the September quarter, this is likely to be just a

temporary reprieve. With a sharp rise in fuel costs over
the past few weeks, inflation is set to reaccelerate back
over 4% before the end of this year, and it’s set to remain
above the RBNZ’s forecast for most of next year.
September monthly prices.
Our forecast for a 0.7% rise in consumer prices in the
September quarter incorporates information from
Stats NZ’s latest monthly prices update. That update
did reveal some softer than expected food price

movements in September. However, that was balanced
against larger than expected rises in airfares and holiday
accommodation costs.

CENTRAL BANK MEETINGS

Bank of Thailand (BOT) (October 28)

We expect the BOT to maintain its policy 
rate at 1.00% at its 28 October meeting, 
extending its pause for a fourth 
consecutive decision as it balances 
inflationary pressures against uneven 
and subdued economic growth. Our view 
is aligned with fixed market income 
pricing. Headline inflation rose anew to 
2.8% yoy in September 2026, driven by 
higher energy, food, and core price 
increases, but remained within the upper 
end of the central bank’s 1 -3% target 
range. As the BOT is still likely to look 
through what it views as temporary, 
supply-driven inflationary pressu res, it 
will likely refrain from tightening policy, 
and keep interest rates accommodative 
to support the soft and uneven growth 
cycle.

European Central Bank (October 29)

We expect the ECB to extend its pause, 
keeping the deposit facility rate at 2.5%. 
Market participants will instead focus on 
the central bank’s assessment of rising 
French and Italian bond yields and 
widening spreads versus Bunds. While 
we do not expect dir ect references to 
individual countries, the ECB is likely to 
signal that recent developments in 
France reflect the country’s weaker 
fiscal and debt profile rather than a 
broader systemic concern. Policymakers 
will seek to differentiate between a 
localised bond sell -off and a Eurozone -
wide market disruption, justifying their 
decision to monitor developments rather 
than implement specific measures at this 
juncture. Meanwhile, inflation continues 
to rise, with little relief expected in 
upcoming readings given elevated 
global fuel prices. 
Bank of Japan (October 30)

The Bank of Japan is expected to keep 
the overnight call rate unchanged at 1.25% 
at this meeting, pausing after the 25bp 
hike in September. The BOJ is likely to 
assess the impact of the previous rate 
hike before taking further action. The 
recent stabilisat ion of the yen also 
reduces the urgency of another hike at 
this meeting.

The tightening bias should remain intact. 
Growth and inflation remain broadly on 
track, leaving room for the BOJ to revise 
up its FY2026 –FY2027 GDP and CPI 
forecasts. Wage growth has also 
remained strong, with Rengo reportedly 
targeting wage increases of 6% in 2027, 
reinforcing the BOJ’s confidence that 
underlying inflation can be sustained 
around its 2% target. We continue to 
expect the policy rate to rise to 1.50% in 
December 2026 or January 2027, 
followed by a further increase to 1.75% 
by 2Q 2027.

FORTHCOMING DATA 
RELEASES

Hong Kong SAR

Export growth is expected to accelerate 
from 53.0% yoy in August to 55.0% in 
September, in line with the pickup in 
China’s export growth from 25.0% to 
36.3%. As a key re-export hub for China,

### Page 3

The Week Ahead October 23, 2026 
                                                                                                                                         3

Hong Kong should benefit from robust 
AI-related electronics trade, with 
electrical machinery, equipment and 
parts exports to Hong Kong accounting 
for 20.6% of China’s total exports. 
Import growth is also expected to remain 
firm, supported by resilient expo rt 
orders and gradually improving 
investment sentiment, sustaining 
demand for intermediate and capital 
goods.

Singapore

Singapore’s industrial production (IP) 
likely remained in expansion for a 13th 
consecutive month in September 2026, 
although we expect growth to moderate 
to 7.6% yoy from August’s strong 15.4% 
yoy pace. IP growth continued to be 
underpinned by the electronics and 
precision engineering clusters, which 
has been key drivers of Singapore’s 
manufacturing strength, reflecting 
sustained demand for artificial 
intelligence-related hardware. However, 
performance across clusters remained 
uneven, with chemicals and biomedical 
manufacturing likely contracting, and 
tempering overall gains.

South Korea

The preliminary estimate for 3Q26 GDP 
is likely to show growth remaining solid 
at 3.2% yoy , slightly below 3.7% in 2Q 
and 3.8% in 1Q. Nominal GDP growth is 
likely to remain in double digits, 
significantly outpacing real GDP growth, 
thanks to improved terms of trade amid 
rising memory export prices. By 
expenditure, exports picked up in 3Q in 
both nominal and real terms, supported 
by stronger semiconductor shipments. 
Investment also appeared robust, as 
reflected in the equipment investment 
estimation index and machinery order 
index. Private consumption, however, 
appeared to weaken amid the sharp 
KOSPI correction since July following  
the introduction of leveraged ETF 
regulations. Our full -year GDP growth 
forecasts of 3.5% for 2026 and 2.4% for 
2027 remain on track.

Taiwan

The preliminary estimate for 3Q GDP is 
likely to show growth remaining strong 
at 12 –13% yoy , close to 12.9% in 2Q, 
albeit below the peak of 15.4% in 1Q. 
Exports maintained strong double -digit 
growth in 3Q (44.6% yoy  in nominal 
terms and 21.1% in real terms), supported 
by robust shipments of semiconductors, 
servers and other AI -related products. 
Machinery investment also sustained 
strong double-digit growth, underpinned 
by higher capacity utilisation in the ICT 
sector and a resulting increase in capital 
goods imports. Private consumption 
appeared to have strengthened further, 
supported by stock market gains, 
improving wage growth, and the 
associated wealth and income effects. 
Construction investment remained the 
weak s pot, weighed down by elevated 
inventories in the new-home market and 
the ongoing need for inventory digestion. 
We maintain our 2026 and 2027 GDP 
growth forecasts at 11.6% and 5.6%, 
respectively, with risks tilted to the 
upside.

## Document 389: 「社外取締役が議長」は何を変えるのか ～プライム市場で118社、大型企業で選任進む～

Source PDF: all report pdf/「社外取締役が議長」は何を変えるのか ～プライム市場で118社、大型企業で選任進む～.pdf
Report source: DLRI
Date: 2026-10-08
Pages: 4
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Flash Insights

2026 年 10 月 8 日

1/4 
「社外取締役が議長」は何を変えるのか 
～プライム市場で 118 社、大型企業で選任進む～ 
 資産運用調査部 シニア研究員  河谷 善夫

1．社外取締役の取締役会議長選任は緩やかに拡大 
筆者は、昨年8 月中旬時点でのプライム市場上場会社のうち、社外取締役を取締役会議長に選
任している会社について分析したレポート（河谷、2025）を公表し、2022 年 7 月上旬時点より、
その数が増加していることを示した。本稿では、2026 年9 月末の時点でのプライム市場における
社外取締役の取締役会議長選任の状況をフォローアップした上で、最後に社外取締役の取締役会
議長選任の意味について考える。

資料 1 社外取締役の取締役会議長選任会社数の推移

（出所）東証公表資料を基に第一ライフ資産運用経済研究所作成

資料 1 のとおり、2025 年 8 月から2026 年 9 月にかけて会社数は9 社増加し、プライム市場での
比率は 6.7％から7.6％と0.9％ポイント上昇した。2022 年以降、年平均で概ね1％ポイントの上
昇ペースが続いている。このグラフだけでは見えないが、2025 年8 月から2026 年9 月にかけて継
続してプライム市場に上場している会社では17 社が社外取締役を新たに取締役会議長に選任して
おり（注1）、新規選任の動きは続いている。 
また、118 名中 117 名が独立社外取締役であり、社外取締役議長はほぼすべて独立社外取締役で
ある（注2）。

### Page 2

2/4 
2．特定の機関設計に限らず普及、ただし社外取締役議長はなお少数 
資料 2 は、会社の機関設計別の2022 年、2025 年、2026 年のプライム市場での社外取締役の取
締役会議長選任会社数と割合を示したものである。

資料 2 機関設計別の社外取締役の取締役会議長選任状況（プライム市場）

（出所）資料 1 と同じ

2026 年では、社外取締役を取締役会議長に選任している会社は、監査役設置会社で5.3％、監
査等委員会設置会社で6.0％、指名委員会等設置会社で42.7％である。指名委員会等設置会社の
割合は依然として高いが、2022 年からの増加状況をみると、社外取締役を取締役会議長とする動
きは、指名委員会等設置会社以外にも徐々に広まっており、昨年のレポートで確認した傾向は続
いているといえる。 
なお、これも資料で示していないが、取締役の過半を独立社外取締役とする会社でも、社外取
締役を取締役会議長とする会社は約2 割にとどまり、2025 年 8 月から2026 年 9 月にかけて大きな
変化はなかった（注3）。

3．社長を議長とする会社の減少と、大型企業で先行する社外取締役議長選任 
資料 3 では、2022 年、2025 年、2026 年のプライム市場での取締役会議長の属性の変化をグラフ
として示した。

資料 3 取締役会議長の属性の変化（プライム市場）

（注）「その他の取締役等」には、会長・社長以外の執行役を兼任する取締役を含む。 
（出所）資料 1 と同じ

### Page 3

3/4 
2022 年からの変化をみると、社長を取締役会議長とする会社の割合は71.4％から65.6％へ
5.8％ポイント低下する一方、社外取締役を議長とする会社の割合は、3.6％から 7.6％へ4.0％ポ
イント上昇し、会長を議長とする会社の割合も1.5％ポイント上昇している。このような社長を取
締役会議長とする会社の割合の低下、社外取締役を議長とする会社の割合の上昇は、取締役会議
長と社長を分離するという、より大きな流れの一部とみることができるだろう。 
次に、どのような会社で社外取締役の取締役会議長選任が進んでいるのかをみるため、2026 年
9 月末時点のプライム市場上場会社について、時価総額ランク別の選任割合を示した（資料4）。

資料 4 時価総額ランク別社外取締役議長選任割合（プライム市場・2026 年 9 月末）

（出所）Bloomberg、東証公表資料データを基に第一ライフ資産運用経済研究所作成

資料 4 からは、時価総額の大きい企業ほど、社外取締役の取締役会議長選任が進んでいるとい
える。なお、社外取締役を取締役会議長に選任している会社の社数の割合は7.6％だが、時価総額
では 19％を占める。また、社外取締役が議長となっている会社の39％が時価総額１兆円以上の会
社である。時価総額が大きい企業ほど選任率が高く、大型企業での社外取締役の取締役会議長選
任が相対的に進んでいる。

4．最後に 
ここまで、社外取締役の取締役会議長選任状況について確認してきた。このような社外取締役
の取締役会議長選任はコーポレートガバナンス上、どのような意味を持つのか、最後に私見を述
べたい。 
とりわけ独立社外取締役が議長を務める場合には、独立した立場から取締役会の議論を促し、
監督機能を含む取締役会の実効性向上につながることが期待される。そのためには、経営陣との
適切な連絡・調整や十分な情報提供が重要となる。今回のコーポレートガバナンス・コード改訂
でも、議長や独立社外取締役を支える取締役会事務局（コーポレートセクレタリー等）の機能強

### Page 4

本資料は情報提供を目的として作成されたものであり、投資勧誘を目的としたものではありません。作成時点で、第一ライフ資産運用
経済研究所が信ずるに足ると判断した情報に基づき作成していますが、その正確性、完全性に対する責任は負いません。見通しは予告
なく変更されることがあります。また、記載された内容は、第一生命保険ないしはその関連会社の投資方針と常に整合的であるとは限
りません。 
4/4 
化の重要性が示されている。社外取締役を議長に選任する企業には、選任によってどのような効
果を目指すのかを明確にし、それに即した支援体制を整えることが重要であろう。 
以 上

【注釈】 
1) 2025 年8 月中旬のプライム市場上場会社（1,618 社）のうち、社外取締役を取締役会議長に選
任している会社数の異同については次のとおり。2026 年 9 月末までの間にプライム市場から退
出した会社（95 社）で社外取締役を取締役会議長に選任していた7 社と、継続上場会社（1,523
社）で社外取締役以外を取締役会議長にした会社1 社の計8 社が減少。継続上場会社のうち17 社
が新規に社外取締役を取締役会議長に選任したので、差し引き9 社の増加となった。新規にプライ
ム市場に上場した会社（20 社）には、社外取締役を取締役会議長に選任していた会社はなかった。 
2) なお、2026 年 9 月末でプライム市場上場会社の全社外取締役数は、6,986 名であり、そのうち
6,793 名（97.2％）が独立社外取締役である。ほとんどの社外取締役は独立社外取締役である
といってよい状況となっている。 
3) 一方、取締役の過半数を独立社外取締役とする会社が着実に増加していることは、河谷
（2026）で示したとおりである。

1
《升息不利地產REITs，但租賃期短者表現較佳》
利率變動會直接牽動房地產投資信託基金 (REITs)的
融資成本增加 ，壓縮可分配現金流與股利 ，依高盛

研究報告顯示，從2006年起的10年期美國國債收益
率上升至少40個基點的滾動六個月週期內，REITs的
表現比標普 500指數低 6.7%，過去五年 ，大多數
REIT 與 10 年期公債殖利率呈負相關，主要因為受
到租賃期間長短的影響 ，其中以飯店業及倉儲設施
因租賃期間較短可當下反應租金變動 ，購物中心因
經濟表現佳 ，可支配所得增加 ，所以表現反而較佳 ；
淨租賃、醫療保健REITs表現最差，但以近期而言，
業績 及營運模式的調整重要性也上升 ，醫療 保健
REITs受個別基本面不同，部分業者營運模式從過去
較長天期的淨租賃轉往養老住宅 ，縮短租賃期，老
年人口財富負擔能力提升，支撐老人住房市場定價

力，租金增加 ，入住率也提升接近 90%；資料中心
中，雖與超大型業者簽訂長約 ，其他機櫃承租客戶
則為短期約 ，但多數繼續續約 ，為長短期混合型租

《預期地產交易價格降溫 ，4Q26交易量增長放緩 》

Green Street CPPI 數據顯示，全美商業地產價格
相較於低點已呈現微幅回升 ，8月上漲0.8%。過去
一年累計上漲5%，但美國國債收益率的上升及對借
貸成本的影響 ，恐促使購房者重新考慮支付意願 ，
預計漲勢即將降溫。依高盛統計截至8月的季度累計
地產交易量年增 18%(扣除合併 )，高於世邦魏理仕
和仲量聯行對 3Q26投資銷售額年增 13%和14%的
預期，8月份的強勁成長主要由工業地產和辦公地產
推動，而多戶住宅市場仍然落後 。工業倉儲主要受
惠供應過剩逐漸下降 ，租賃活動受到美國製造業回
流、近岸外包與第三方物流供應鏈重組支撐 ，優質
近港與門戶型物流中心空置率開始回落；辦公室物
業則呈現科技與專業服務企業偏好承租一線 A級商
辦，而不青睞 B/C級商辦 ，拆除舊型辦公室 、改建
住宅等 。多戶住宅仍在消化陽光帶 (德州、佛州等)
過剩的存量，壓抑短期租金漲幅 。受30年期固定房
貸利率在9月升至7%的高位及去年基期也高的影響 ，
預期4Q26交易量放緩。

《REITs股息吸引力低，轉注意現金流成長動能》
美REITs的回報在利率下降時會擴張估值，但現美國
10年期公債殖利率多 在5%以上，美國REITs平均股

持中立。然若考量租金現金流增長 ，租金定價力相
對強勢的族群主要為養老住宅 ，同店淨營利年增率
可達20%上下，但估值偏高，建議回檔可留意。

中國兩年期殖利率 1.25% 景氣下行壓力持續，本行仍預期接下來有降準50bp空間。
新興市場股 中立 油價高檔拉鋸，主要國家央行貨幣政策偏向緊縮，債務堆壘隱憂揮之不去，高利環境下，兼具基本面穩健與政策操作有空間的新亞、拉美相對正向。
新興亞洲 中立 新興亞洲中相對看好台灣、韓國、東協與印度，AI推論的供不應求，適時留意評價面空間循序參與台韓投資契機，印度有評價面保護優勢。

中國A 中立 中國景氣K型化加劇，投資方向維持選股不選市，聚焦受惠AI相關供應鏈兼國產替代政策扶植的科技族群、稀缺原物料及高附加出口價值等產業。
香港 中立 削價競爭雖告尾聲但內需乏力影響獲利，並關注資本支出轉為獲利進程。
台灣 正向 長線台股AI供應鏈握技術進程優勢與漲價潮，擇評價面分批布局台股。

3
• 8月中國經濟數據仍顯示
外貿強 ，內需疲軟的景
象。除反應信心不足 、
內需疲軟外 ，反內卷的

• 中國經濟現階段來自價
格為主的出口貢獻 ，景

期，內需疲軟將視財政
